115年 6月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
6 月實徵淨額 1 兆 1,656 億元，較上年同月增加 7,618 億元(+1.9 倍)，
主因去年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長 ，稅款遞延至 7 月所致。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額 2 兆 5,876 億元，較上年同期增加 1 兆 1,623
億元(+81.5%)。 
（三）達成率 
累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 124.9%，占全年預算數
65.6%。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）6 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以綜合所得稅增加 3,468 億元、營利
事業所得稅增加 3,404 億元及證券交易稅增加 624 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以營利事業所得稅增加 4,997
億元、綜合所得稅增加 3,819 億元及證券交易稅增加 2,181 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-6 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
6 月實徵淨額 5,883 億元，較上年同月增加 3,404 億元(+1.4 倍)，累計
1 至 6 月實徵淨額 7,803 億元，較上年同期增加 4,997 億元(+1.8 倍)，
主因上年為因應美國對等關稅帶來之衝擊，所得稅結算申報及繳納期
限延長，稅款遞延至 7 月入帳，以及今年結算申報自繳稅款增加。
2.綜合所得稅
6 月實徵淨額 4,050 億元，較上年同月增加 3,468 億元(+6.0 倍)，主
因上年結算申報及繳納期限延長；累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額 6,709 億
元，較上年同期增加 3,819 億元(+1.3 倍)，除基期因素外，亦受結算

申報自繳稅款增加影響。
3.營業稅
(1)6月因資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口代徵稅額增加 ，營業稅
實徵淨額較上年同月增加62億元，由負105億元縮小為負44億元。
(2)累計1至6月實徵淨額3,496億元，較上年同期增加587億元(+20.2%)，
主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口代徵稅額增加，
惟適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

3
4.貨物稅
6月實徵淨額108億元，較上年同月減少1億元(-1.0%)，主因進口車及
油氣類稅額增減互抵所致；累計1至6月實徵淨額579億元，較上年同
期減少158億元(-21.4%)，主因新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加，以及汽柴油減徵幅度擴大。
5.證券交易稅
(1)6 月及累計 1 至 6 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 5,407 億
元、1 兆 1,738 億元，分別較上年同期(4,328 億元、4,038 億元)增
2.6 倍、1.9 倍。
(2)6 月證交稅實徵淨額 849 億元，較上年同月增加 624 億元(+2.8 倍)；
累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額 3,336 億元，較上年同期增加 2,181 億元(+1.9
倍)。
6.關稅
6 月實徵淨額 152 億元，較上年同月增加 30 億元(+24.6%)，主因小
客車進口稅額增加；累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額 784 億元，較上年同期
增加 37 億元(+4.9%)，主因機械及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進

口稅額增加。
7.遺產稅
6 月實徵淨額 45 億元，較上年同月增加 16 億元(+58.1%)，累計 1 至
6 月實徵淨額 259 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+45.2%)，均因大
額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
6 月實徵淨額 55 億元，較上年同月減少 2 億元(-3.8%)，累計 1 至 6
月實徵淨額 332 億元，較上年同期減少 29 億元(-8.1%)，主因房地交
易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.房屋稅
6 月實徵淨額 363 億元，較上年同月減少 6 億元(-1.6%)，主因 114
年配合房屋稅 2.0 實施首年，放寬房屋使用情形申報期限至 6 月 2
日，繳納期限同步順延所致；累計 1 至 6 月實徵淨額 983 億元，較
上年同期增加 39 億元(+4.2%)，主因開徵件數增加。
（三）前 6 月主要徵起稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(275%)、遺產稅(162%)、贈與稅(128%)、印花
稅(127%)、綜所稅(126%)、營所稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(109%)、

營業稅(108%)、房屋稅(106%)、牌照稅(101%)及菸酒稅(100%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(99.9%)、地價稅(88%)、土增稅(84%)及特銷稅
(53%)等。

三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 6 月中央政府賦稅收入(不含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵淨額 1
兆 7,933 億元(占分配預算數 136.2%)，較上年同期增加 8,371 億元
(+87.5%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 1,575 億元(-90.9%)，惟所得稅因繳納期間差異及今
年結算申報自繳稅款成長，增加 7,802 億元(+1.6 倍)，證交稅亦大幅增
加 2,181 億元(+1.9 倍)，故互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 6 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵
淨額 7,378 億元(占分配預算
數 108.8%)，較上年同期增加
3,296 億元(+80.8%)，主因納入
中央統籌分配 之營業稅 增加
2,241 億元(+2.0 倍)，以及所得
稅增加 1,027 億元(+1.8 倍)所
致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 6 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 131 億元，較

上年同期增加 2 億元(+1.6%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 40
億元，較上年同期減少 3 億元(-6.1%)；撥入長照基金 393 億元(其中 160
億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、131 億元來自菸稅，102 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 35 億元(+9.8%)。

2026 年 7 月 31 日 全8 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 6 月雇用統計 
失業率は2.5％と前月から横ばい、就業者数は減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年 6 月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.5％と、前月から横ばいだった。失業者
数は小幅ながら3 カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋1 万人）した。他方、就業者数は3 カ月ぶ
りに減少（同▲36 万人）したが、4、5 月に上振れしていた反動とみられ、高水準を維
持している。総じて見れば、雇用環境は前月から概ね横ばいで推移したとみられる。 
◼ 2026 年6 月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.18 倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と4 カ月ぶり
に小幅に上昇した。新規求人倍率は2.16 倍（同＋0.05pt）と前月から上昇した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、下振れリスクは小さくない。中東情勢を巡る不確実性が依然と
して高い状況が続いており、原油の価格高騰や供給不足が長期化すれば、企業収益の
悪化を通じて雇用調整が進む恐れがある。トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トラン
プ関税）や日中関係の悪化の影響にも引き続き注意が必要だ。

図表 1：雇用関連指標の推移

6 月の完全失業率：2.5％と前月から横ばい 
2026 年6 月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.5％と前月から横ばいだった（図表2 左上） 。失
業者数は小幅ながら 3 カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋1 万人）した。他方、就業者数は 3 カ月ぶり
に減少（同▲36 万人）したが、4、5 月に上振れしていた反動とみられ、高水準を維持してい
る。総じて見れば、雇用環境は前月から概ね横ばいで推移したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、いずれも小幅な変化だった。「自発的な離職による者」 （前
月差＋1 万人）と「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」(同＋1 万人)は小幅に増加した。「定年又
は雇用契約の満了」は横ばいだった。他方、 「新たに求職」 （同▲2 万人）は小幅に減少した。
（巻末の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数を業種別に見ると、「学術研究，専門・技術サービス業」 や「宿泊業，飲食サービ
ス業」を中心に、その他の非製造業が 大きく減少し た（図表 2 左下）。「卸売業，小売業」や
「医療，福祉」も減少した。他方、「製造業」、「建設業」、「情報通信業」は増加した。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は3 カ月連続で増加（前月差＋6
万人）し、非正規雇用者は2 カ月連続で減少（同▲21 万人）した（図表2 右下）。 
図表 2：就業者数・完全失業率（左上） 、非労働力人口・労働参加率（右上） 、 
業種別就業者数（左下） 、雇用形態別雇用者数（右下）

（注）業種別就業者数のみ大和総研による季節調整値で、その他は総務省による季節調整値。

6 月の新規求人倍率：求職は減少した一方で求人は増加し、前月から上昇 
2026 年 6 月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.18 倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と 4 カ月ぶりに小
幅に上昇した。新規求人倍率も 2.16 倍（同＋0.05pt）と前月から上昇した（図表3 左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数（前月比＋0.9％）は 3 カ月連続で増加し、新規求人数
（同＋1.0％）は 2 カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 4 左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同▲0.1％）
は 3 カ月ぶりに減少し、新規求職申込件数（同▲1.1％）は2 カ月連続で減少した。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.00 倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と
わずかながら 15 カ月ぶりに上昇した。新規求人倍率は 1.76 倍（同＋0.05pt）と 3 カ月ぶりに
上昇した（図表 3 右）。 
図表 3：有効求人倍率と新規求人倍率（左：全数、右：正社員）

（注）季節調整値。

率、有効求人倍率で除すことで算出。

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢などの下振れリスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が7 月 3 日に公表した第 7 回（最終）回答集
計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率 （加重平均）が 企業規 模計で 5.01％と、前年
（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している 0F
1。300 人未満の中小組合では 4.69％と
前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。 
ただし、下振れリスクは小さくない。とりわけ中東情勢の影響には注意が必要だ。6 月 17 日
に米国とイランが戦闘終結等に関する覚書に署名したものの、両国間ではその後も攻撃の応酬
が繰り返されている。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況にある。 
覚書が履行されることでホルムズ海峡の安全な航行が確保されることが期待されるが、原油
等の供給網の正常化には時間を要するとみられる。そのため、 輸入原油価格の高止まりや、必
要量の石油関連製品の確保が困難な状況がしばらく続く可能性がある。特に原油高は価格上昇
が広く波及するため、企業収益の悪化を通じて幅広い業界で雇用調整が進む恐れがある。さら
に、先行きが不透明な状況が長引けば、企業が採用活動を縮小することも懸念される。 
中東情勢のほか、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2を受け、対米輸出への
悪影響が拡大する恐れがある。さらに、日中関係の悪化が長期化・深刻化するリスクも懸念さ
れる。中国人訪日客数の回復の遅れや 2F
3、レアアース（希土類）などの調達難 3F
4が発生する可能
性がある。これらの要因が労働需要を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3 年連続の 5％超え！～2026 春季生活闘争 第7 回（最終）回答集計結果
について～」（2026 年7 月3 日） 
2 トランプ米政権は 7 月 24 日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発
動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026 年7 月24 日））。 
3 詳細は、山口茜「インバウンドに忍び寄る外部ショック」（大和総研レポート、2026 年4 月9 日）を参照。 
4 中国政府は 6 月 29 日、軍民両用（デュアルユース）品目の対日輸出禁止の対象リストを、防衛関連企業を中
心に 40 の企業・団体に拡大した（日本経済新聞 電子版「中国、軍民両用品の対日輸出禁止第 2 弾 三菱電
機系や日鋼特機など」（2026 年 6 月29 日））。

市場焦點
市場反覆評估AI需求前景
美國
• 美國6月非農就業新增 5.7萬人，明顯低於市場預期的 11萬人，亦較5月下修後的 12.9萬人進
一步降溫，創下今年以來最低增幅。失業率則由4.3%降至4.2%，但主因勞動參與率下滑所致，
並非就業市場顯著改善。
• 儘管通膨數據顯示核心物價仍在可控範圍 ，但利率期貨市場仍押注今年可能升息 1~2碼，反
映市場真正擔心的可能不是通膨壓力 ，而是新任Fed主席華許的貨幣政策立場 。在市場消化
完「不確定性」前，股市恐隨著消息大幅波動 ，建議不妨逢修正加碼長期前景佳的優質股票 。
中國
• 隨著美伊衝突對全球供應鏈的干擾減弱 ，中國6月製造業PMI顯示出口韌性持續支撐生產，展
望7月，高溫恐干擾施工與工業生產，加上部分製造業進入設備檢修，製造業PMI將面臨季節

性回落壓力，惟6月景氣回升已建立於訂單改善基礎，AI伺服器、半導體與電腦出口動能仍強，
且基建投資具支撐力，預期擴張動能可望部分延續，中國整體製造業景氣維持溫和成長。
日股
• 日本官方與民間將攜手合作投資 17個戰略領域，預估至2040年，投資總額將突破370兆日圓，
除了市場矚目的 AI與半導體外，包含造船等大宗傳統製造業也名列其中 ，將帶來跨板塊的基
建與轉型投資機會 。在政策與基本面的雙重加持下 ，對於看好全球AI投資循環與日本企業獲
利改善的投資人，可藉由分散布局、定期定額的方式，參與日股的長線成長。

油價降提振美國消費者信心，惟就業降溫恐牽動後續消費動能
• 油價回穩與景氣預期改善，帶動消費者信心回升：
美國6月諮商會消費者信心指數升至91.2，高於5
月90.6，主因能源價格回落、中東地緣政治風險降
溫，加上民眾對景氣與家庭財務前景轉趨樂觀，使
整體信心改善，惟指數仍低於歷史平均，反映消費
者對經濟前景仍抱持審慎態度。
• 勞動市場降溫，消費支出仍須持續觀察：現況指數
降至116.4，就業評價持續轉弱，認為「工作難找」
比例升至22.5%，創2021年初以來新高，顯示企
業招募需求放緩。若就業市場持續降溫，可能影響
薪資成長與消費支出，進而牽動下半年經濟動能。
• 後市觀察：油價回落有助舒緩通膨壓力，消費信心
回穩亦降低市場對經濟衰退的疑慮；然而，多數消
費者仍預期利率將維持相對高檔，加上勞動市場已

有降溫跡象，後續消費力道仍待觀察。建議採取
「成長搭配防禦」配置策略，可布局美國大型AI科
技股掌握企業獲利成長，並搭配全球多元收益基金，
兼顧收益與波動管理。

美國製造業景氣維持擴張，成本壓力降溫支撐景氣
• 製造業連六個月擴張，企業投資動能仍具韌性：美國6月ISM製造業PMI由前月54.0降至53.3，雖略低於市場預期，但仍
連續六個月高於50，顯示製造業維持擴張。新訂單與生產指數持續處於擴張區間 ，反映企業資本支出及AI基礎建設投資
仍支撐製造業活動；惟出口與訂單成長趨緩，顯示關稅及地緣政治不確定性開始影響需求。
• 投入成本降溫，就業改善仍待確認： 價格指數由82.1降至73.0，顯示能源價格回穩帶動成本壓力緩解；就業指數回升至
49.7，但仍低於50，而企業招募態度仍偏保守，意味製造業景氣已由快速復甦轉向穩健擴張。
• 後市觀察：ISM數據顯示美國製造業景氣延續復甦 ，但成長動能已由高峰轉趨溫和 ，後續仍須觀察就業 、通膨及Fed政

策方向，建議投資人持續布局受惠AI資本支出與企業數位化趨勢的半導體 、伺服器、自動化設備及工業科技龍頭 ，同時
搭配投資級債與高品質股息資產，以兼顧景氣循環受惠機會及投資組合穩定性。

美6月非農就業僅新增5.7萬人，升息疑慮緩解
• 非農新增就業放緩，惟勞動市場未出現明顯惡化跡象：美國6月非農就業新增5.7萬人，明顯低於市場預期的11萬人，亦較5
月下修後的12.9萬人進一步降溫，創下今年以來最低增幅。失業率則由4.3%降至4.2%，但主因勞動參與率下滑所致，並非
就業市場顯著改善。整體而言，本次非農數據反映勞動市場由先前偏熱逐步回歸正常化，雖招聘動能放緩，但尚未出現急劇
惡化跡象，亦與近期小型企業招聘計畫等調查所反映的趨緩趨勢一致。相符。
• 勞動市場屬降溫而非急劇惡化，Fed關注焦點仍將側重通膨：非農報告顯示，美國勞動市場持續朝溫和成長調整，企業招募
態度轉趨審慎，但薪資成長仍具支撐力，加上近期JOLTS職缺數及ISM就業指數同步改善，顯示整體勞動需求依然穩健。就

業市場適度降溫，有助市場降低對Fed進一步升息的預期，惟在通膨壓力尚未完全消退下，聯準會後續政策仍將以通膨數據
及勞動市場變化作為主要決策依據。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.7.3

平均
Fed立場不確定性圍繞 留意市場波動下的布局機會

• 聯準會主席交接 ，政策不確定性干擾市場： 歷史經驗顯示 ，每逢聯準會(Fed)迎來新主席 ，標普500指數在其上任後短期面
臨修正壓力。主要原因並非經濟基本面立即惡化，而是市場對貨幣政策路徑缺乏明確預期，這段期間市場會對新任主席的發
言進行檢視，只要對政策立場的解讀發生轉變，就會引發強烈的不確定性並導致股市回檔。
• 通膨數據方法論修正 ，PCE下修或緩和Fed鷹派立場： 美國商務部經濟分析局計劃調整個人消費支出物價指數 (PCE)的計算
方法論，主因在於通膨細項中的 「投資組合管理費 」在近幾個月大幅攀升 ，造成核心PCE與核心CPI之間異常擴大差距 ，資
料預計回溯至2021年，並於今年9月生效。市場預測5月份核心PCE將小幅下修，有望降低聯準會進一步緊縮的可能性。
• 觀點：儘管通膨數據顯示核心物價仍在可控範圍，且多位經濟學家認為美國的通膨數據出現見頂跡象，但利率期貨市場仍押
注今年可能升息1~2碼，反映市場真正擔心的可能不是通膨壓力 ，而是新任Fed主席華許的貨幣政策立場 。對於投資市場來

說「不確定性」大幅上升，在市場消化完成之前，股市恐隨著消息大幅波動，建議不妨逢修正加碼長期前景佳的優質股票。
資料來源：Bloomberg，兆豐銀行整理。
歷任聯準會主席上任後3個月美股最大回撤
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.7.1。
彭博預測5月份核心PCE將下修13個百分點

日本政府針對AI及半導體領域，制定了370兆日元的投資計劃
• 日本370兆日圓投資，催化多元產業轉型契機：日本
政府於2026年6月底召開經濟財政諮詢會議與成長戰
略會議的聯合會議，會中指出，日本官方將與民間攜
手將合作投資17個戰略領域中選定的62項產品與技術，
預估至2040年，投資總額將突破370兆日圓，除了市
場矚目的AI與半導體外，包含造船等大宗傳統製造業
也名列其中，將帶來跨板塊的基建與轉型投資機會。
• 日本佔據AI供應鏈上游關鍵地位：日本企業佔據半導
體上游設備與關鍵原物料的重要地位，隨AI技術持續
快速演進，全球對高階晶片與硬體基建的需求有增無
減，使日本相關供應鏈企業的長期獲利前景維持強勁，
其投資機會的範圍也在進一步擴大（從純半導體設備
延伸至電力基建、散熱等泛AI概念）。

• 小結：在政策與基本面的雙重加持下，創造多元投資
機會。對於看好全球AI投資循環與日本企業獲利改善
的投資人，可藉由分散布局、定期定額的方式，參與
日股的長線成長。

中國製造業PMI顯示出口韌性持續支撐生產
• 中國製造業PMI顯示出口韌性持續支撐生產： 隨著美伊衝突對全球供應鏈的干擾減弱 ，中國出口表現持續展現韌性 ，對製造業
形成重要支撐，6月製造業PMI由5月的51.8回落至51.7，雖略低於市場預期 ，但仍在榮枯線之上 ，反映整體景氣維持擴張趨勢 ，
其中產出與新訂單指數持續位於擴張區間 ，此外既有數據亦顯示出口動能穩健 ，包括出口海運費用及對美貨櫃發運量皆為雙位
數成長，另一方面在油價下行帶動下 ，原物料成本漲勢放緩 ，有助於企業利潤修復 ，整體而言，製造業在外需支撐與成本壓力
緩解的雙重作用下，短期景氣有望維持溫和擴張。
• 展望後市：7月高溫恐干擾施工與工業生產 ，加上部分製造業進入設備檢修 ，製造業PMI將面臨季節性回落壓力 ，惟6月景氣回

升已建立於訂單改善基礎 ，AI伺服器、半導體與電腦出口動能仍強 ，且基建投資具支撐力 ，預期擴張動能可望部分延續 ，整體
製造業景氣維持溫和成長。

中國工業企業量價齊揚，帶動利潤改善
• 中國工業企業量價齊揚，帶動利潤改善：今年1至5月，中國規模以上工業企業累計利潤總額約人民幣 3.14兆元，年增18.8%，
增速較1至4月提升0.6個百分點，顯示整體工業部門獲利動能持續 ，獲利改善主因為生產端維持穩健擴張；另一方面 PPI價格
回升，帶動出廠價格改善，也進一步推升企業利潤空間 。產業面觀察，上游原物料與AI相關產業為主要成長動能 ，受大宗商
品價格走升影響，採礦業與原材料製造業分別貢獻工業利潤增速 4.4及7.9個百分點，另一方面，在生成式AI與高效運算需求
推動下，算力與記憶體相關需求快速爆發 ，帶動電子產業獲利顯著上升 ，1至5月利潤年增達103.9%，成為整體工業獲利成
長的核心來源，最後則是在新能源應用擴展及 AI需求帶動下，有色金屬需求強勁，銅、鋁價格處於相對高檔，亦對整體企業

獲利提供正向貢獻。
• 總結來說：目前工業企業獲利回升，呈現上游價格支撐以及AI需求驅動，當前產業升級趨勢明確，逐步朝高附加價值與科技
導向發展，中國科技自主發展後市看好。
中國工業企業收入和利潤增速表現

全球股票估值極端分化
• 台灣領銜全球股市估值，超越美國成全球最貴市場：在AI樂觀情緒的強烈推動下，2026年全球股市的估值重估呈現極端不均
衡的狀態，投資者將資金高度集中於被視為科技贏家的市場。台股在今年經歷了大幅重估，預期本益比飆升25%至23倍，不
論從絕對值還是過去10年的歷史區間來看 ，已正式超越美國(20.8倍)，成為全球估值最高的主要市場 。相比之下，日本股市
(17.5倍)雖高於歷史均值，但重估幅度較小；而韓國 (預期本益比僅7.2倍)與中國大陸股市則處於深度的折價狀態 。產業層面
上，科技與金融板塊享有全市場最高的估值溢價，反映市場對成長潛力和獲利韌性的偏好。
• 全球預期本益比與市淨率 vs. ROE：全球股票平均預期本益比為17.8倍，台灣與印度的長條圖區間及當前點位明顯高居新興

與發達市場前列；而韓國與中國則處於長條圖的底端 (韓國僅7.2倍)，形成巨大的估值差異。雖然台灣的ROE在全球名列前茅，
但其市淨率已高出當前盈利能力所暗示的公允價值 40%以上，溢價率與日本並列全球最大 。反之，巴西、墨西哥、南非與中
國則存在顯著折價，中國股市的公允價值可能較目前市淨率高出21%，高增長市場固然迷人，但短期內價格已顯著透支。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.6.29

貨幣與資金流向
• 美國經濟例外論與鷹派央行提振美元多頭： 隨著地緣政治局勢逐步緩和 ，外匯在2026年下半年重回經濟週期與殖利率因素
驅動。美國年初經濟基本面強勁且具備石油淨出口國優勢 ，面對高油價與停滯性通膨壓力比歐 、英、日更具韌性。聯準會
新任主席釋放明確偏鷹信號，市場預期2026年底聯邦基金利率將調升至4.08%，因而驅動美元看漲。
• 8兆美元現金囤積：當前美股雖創下歷史新高 ，但全球投資人一邊擁抱風險資產 ，一邊卻將高達8兆美元的巨額資金停留在
無風險的貨幣市場基金及家庭存款中 。主因：(1)貨幣市場基金提供近4%無風險報酬，極具吸引力。(2)自2022年傳統股債
負相關失效後，投資人進而轉向現金作為替代。目前貨幣市場基金資產約占整基金規模24%(超越債券基金18%)，但僅相當

於美股總市值11%(低於先前16%峰值)，顯示股市市值成長速度仍快於現金餘額增速。貨幣市場基金規模與聯邦基金利率關
係顯示利率維持接近4%高位，貨幣型基金規模越高，一旦利率向3%回落，這8兆美元的隱形買盤將有望湧入股市延續牛市 。
• 總結及建議： 全球正處於高估值與高防禦現金並存的極端雙軌環境 。一方面，AI狂熱催生台灣 、美國等科技板塊的高度溢
價；另一方面，接近4%的無風險利率與對地緣經濟的擔憂，讓投資人牢牢踩著現金不放，近期留意高估值市場股票回檔。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
사상 최대 실적은 진행형 
SK하이닉스 분기 사상 최대 실적은 연내 지속될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 2분기 실적에 
대해서는 시황에 부합한 좋은 실적으로 평가한다. 지나치게 높은 예상치로 컨센서스가 
높아지긴 했지만 제품 믹스를 제대로 예측하지 못한 오류로 인한 영향이다. DRAM 및 
NAND ASP가 경쟁사 대비 낮게 상승한 것은 제품 믹스가 다른 결과이다. 26년 3분기 
영업이익을 79.6조원으로 전망한다. 이전 전망치를 상회한 수준이다. 
시장이 걱정하는 위기는 오지 않을 것 
주식시장은 같은 변수에 대한 해석이 분위기에 따라 달라진다. 유가, 금리, 환율이 
모두 그렇다. 반도체 주가는 업황, 실적을 보고 싶어하지 않는다. 조금이라도 흠집 
낼만한 이슈에 대해서 과한 수준으로 반응하고 있다. 과거의 경험에서 근거로 찾고 
있다. 빠르게 상승한 주가가 가장 큰 이유이다. 걱정한 것처럼 실적이 나빠질 
것으로 전망하지 않는다. 수요가 일시적이지 않고, 상당기간 증가세를 유지할 
것으로 기대하기 때문이다. 공급 증가는 상당기간 제한적으로 증가할 수 밖에 없다. 
AI 투자가 일시적인 현상이 아니라는 것은 확인될 변수이다. 
목표주가를 바꿀 이유를 찾을 수 없다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 목표주가를 상향한 이후 주가는 가파르게 하락했다. 펀더멘탈을 
건드리는 여러 변수에 반응한 수급이 원인으로 해석한다. 우려의 진위 여부는 시간을 
거쳐서 확인되겠지만, 펀더멘탈이 변화될 만한 변수는 아니라고 판단하고, 실적 
추정치를 상향 조정했다. 목표주가 400만원은 12개월 예상 EPS 416,692원에 9.6배 
수준이다. 높아진 ROE, 영업적자가 반복되는 시기가 아닌 이상 굳이 PBR 밴드로 
밸류에이션 한계를 지을 이유도 없다고 판단한다.

 SK하이닉스 (000660) 
 실적과 어긋난 주가, 한번은 거칠 과정

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 2분기, 이변 없는 사상 최대 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 2분기 매출액은 26년 1분기 대비 50.9% 증가한 79조 3,190억원이다.  
이전 전망과 유사한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 전망과 대부분 
유사하고 NAND ASP는 예상치를 상회한 수준이다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 1분기 대비 43.1% 증가했다. B/G는 +6.7%, ASP는 +31%로 
추정했다. 2분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비로는 낮은 수준이다. 경쟁사 대비 
HBM 비중이 높은 것과 낮은 모바일 비중, 2 분기에 가격 변화가 없는 계약 등이 
복합적으로 미친 영향으로 분석된다. HBM은 15% 수준으로 추정한다. HBM4 규모는 
3분기부터 빠르게 증가할 전망이다. 
NAND 매출액은 26년 1분기 대비 81% 증가했다. B/G 는 +15%, ASP는 +54%로 
추정한다. ASP는 이전 전망 대비 높은 수준이다. 경쟁사 대비 가격 상승폭이 낮은 
것은 SSD 비중이 높기 때문이다. 2분기 SSD 비중은 76%이다.  
26년 2분기 영업이익은 26년 1분기 대비 61% 증가한 60.5조원이다. 이전 전망과 
유사한 수준이다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.8%p 상승한 76.3%이다. DRAM 
영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 46.8% 증가한 45.78조원으로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.0%p 상승한 79.0%로 추정한다. NAND 영업이익은 전 분기 대비 2배 
이상 증가한 14.8조원으로 전망한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 14.5%p 높아진 
69.5%로 예상한다.

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 23.2% 증가한 97.7조원으로 
예상한다. 가격은 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 것으로 예상하고, 물량은 2분기 
대비 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 3분기 영업이익은 제품별 이익률 개선으로 26년 2분기 
대비 31.4% 증가한 79.6조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 61.2조원, NAND 18.4조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 25.7% 증가한 72.86조원으로 예상한다. 이전 전망 
대비 상향한 수준이다. ASP 는 +16%, Bit Growth 는 +10%로 가정하였다. HBM 
출하는 지난 분기 대비 소폭 증가할 것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 30%대로 높아질 
전망이다. 
26년 3분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.3% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +2%로 전망하고. ASP는 +20%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환으로 근근히 수요 대응 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 2분기에는 10%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 대응할 것으로 예상한다. 2026년 DRAM 수요 Bit 
Growth는 26.6%이다.  
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L 는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 2분기 321L 
비중은 40%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
혹독한 시련을 불러온 변수에 대한 올바른 해석 
1. AI 투자 성장률 둔화 : 어디에 투자하는 지를 살펴 보자 
AI 투자의 증가율이 둔화 된다는 것이 메모리 투자가 축소된다는 것을 의미하는 것이 
아니다. Agent AI에서는 CPU와 DRAM/NAND에 대한 필요성이 더 높아지게 된다. 
그래서 일반 서버 투자 비중이 높아지게 된다. 과거에 비싸게 확보했던 High End 
GPU 서버 1개 비용으로 일반 서버 40개를 구매할 수 있다. 결국 메모리 수요는 꾸준
히 증가하는 것으로 봐야 한다.   
2. 효율적 AI 모델 출현 : 컴퓨팅 파워는 필연적으로 동반 
효율적 AI 모델이 중국에서만 꾸준히 등장하고 있다. 저비용 고효율을 강조한다. 저비
용인 이유는 Major AI 모델처럼 Training AI 관련 투자를 생략했기 때문이다. 그럼에
도 불구하고 모델을 가동하기 위해서 CPU, DRAM과 같은 H/W 투자는 필수적이다. 
Token, Parmeter는 모두 메모리 수요를 동반하기 때문이다.  
3. 공급자가 만드는 시장은 허수일까 : 과거와 다른 투자 형태일 뿐 
NVIDIA는 최근 전략적 파트너에 대한 투자를 확대하고 있다. AI 모델 개발사인 SSI, 
네오클라우드 사업자인 Coreweave, 데이터센터업체인 IREN, Hut8, 광통신업체인 
Coherent, Lumentum 등이 대표적 사례이다. 이는 AI 시스템에서 주역이 되려는 
NVIDIA의 전략으로 해석해야 한다. NVIDIA뿐만 아니라 Meta의 Black Rock과 같은 
투자 파트너는 더 늘어날 것으로 전망한다. 
4. 투자 계획을 고려할 때 공급 과잉이 올 것이다 : 수요를 고려해서 투자한다 
2분기 중에 발표한 메모리 업체들의 장기 투자 규모는 이례적으로 큰 규모이다. 수요
가 빠르게 성장하는 것에 대응하는 것으로 해석한다. 수요 상황을 무시하고 무분별하
게 투자한다면 공급 과잉을 피할 수 없겠지만 수요를 확인하지 않고 무작정 투자를 하
지는 않는다. 이를 담보하기 위해서 LTA를 체결하는 것이다. 
5. 중국발 공급 과잉 : 지나 보면 허수 
CXMT가 상장했다. DRAM 메이커 중에서 가장 높은 밸류에이션을 받고 있다. 중국 
시가총액 1위에 자리매김 하고 있다. 상장 후 Capa 증설에 대한 우려가 있지만 상장 
후 확보한 자금의 15%인 1.6조원이 Wafer 확대에 투자되고 나머지는 R&D에 사용할 
것으로 발표했다. 의미 없는 생산량 확대는 필요 없다고 판단한 것으로 해석한다. 선
도업체를 영원히 따라 잡지 못하지는 않겠지만 상당기간은 어려울 것으로 전망한다.

GPU가 아닌 메모리 
비중이 높아진다 
효율적 모델이어도 
메모리는 필요하다 
누가 투자하는지가 아니라 
왜 투자하는지가 변수 
누구도 공급 과잉을 
바라지 않는다 
중국은 걱정하지 말자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 4,000,000원 유지 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 2026년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 7월 중 극심하게 조정 받은 주가도 적극적인 투자의 요인으로 판단한다. 
목표주가는 4,000,000원을 유지한다. 2분기에 급격하게 상승한 여파로 큰 폭의 하락세가 
지속되지만 주가 하락을 자극했던 변수들은 시간이 지나면서 부정적으로 해석할 변수가 
아니라는 것이 판명될 것으로 전망한다. 
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. Hyperscaler들의 투자가 과하고 수익성 창출로 연결되기 
어려운 구조로 인식되는 것도 불식시킬 변수라 판단한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

주: K-IFRS연결요약재무제표
[투자포인트]
■ 현대차 26.2Q 매출액 48.0억원 (-0.5% YoY), 영업이익 2.8조원 (-20.1% YoY, 6.0% OPM) 전망. 현대차
26.2Q 국내 도매 판매의 경우 158,515대 (-15.9% YoY)를 기록. 안전 공업 화재 관련 생산 차질 영향이
존재하고, 그룹사 대비 생산 차질 영향이 주력 차종에 집중된 것으로 파악. 26.06 현대차 국내 출고 대수는
60,367대를 기록했고, 26.3Q부터 생산 차질 영향은 미미할 것. 기말 환율이 상승함에 따른 판매보증충당금

추가 인식분이 2,000억원 내외 발생할 전망이고, 원자재 가격 상승 영향도 여전히 존재하는 것으로 파악됨.
■ 26.2Q 미국 도매 판매 265,208대 (+2.0% YoY)를 기록. 전년 동기 대비 미국 판매 성장폭이 감소하고 있는
것은 사실이고, 인센티브 상승 압박도 상당한 것으로 파악됨. 단 26.03 일부 트림에서의 리콜 사태가 발생한
펠리세이드 판매 업황이 준수하다는 점이 인상적. 26.2Q 펠리세이드 미국 판매 대수는 35,938대 (+26.5%
YoY)를 기록. 이는 26.2H 출시 예정된 볼룸 모델 신차 효과와 함께 올해의 성장 동인이 될 것으로 전망.
■ 현대차 기업가치는 1) 본업, 2) SDV/자율주행 경쟁력, 3) Boston Dynamics 등 세가지 요소로 구성되고, 이 중
Boston Dynamics의 비중이 높다는 점은 분명. 연초부터 주가 급등 국면이 관찰되었던 이유는 시장이 Boston

Dynamics 경쟁력과 향후 사업 로드맵에 공감하였기 때문. 시장 변동성이 확대되며 주가 조정 국면이 이어지고
있으나, 28년 양산 공장 완공 및 RMAC 가동 등의 주요 로드맵은 견조함에 따라 목표주가 650,000원을 유지.

Compliance notice
당보고서공표일기준으로해당기업과관련하여,
회사는해당종목을1%이상보유하고있지않습니다.
금융투자분석사와그배우자는해당기업의주식을보유하고있지않습니다.
당보고서는기관투자가및제3자에게E-mail등을통하여사전에배포된사실이없습니다.

회사는6개월간해당기업의유가증권발행과관련주관사로참여하지않았습니다.
당보고서에게재된내용들은본인의의견을정확하게반영하고있으며,외부의부당한압력이나간섭없이
작성되었음을확인합니다.
본분석자료는투자자의증권투자를돕기위한참고자료이며,따라서,본자료에의한투자자의투자결과에대해
어떠한목적의증빙자료로도사용될수없으며,어떠한경우에도작성자및당사의허가없이전재,복사또는
대여될수없습니다.무단전재등으로인한분쟁발생시법적책임이있음을주지하시기바랍니다.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 2분기, ESS가 예상 대비 부진 
LG에너지솔루션의 2Q26 매출액 7조 5,600억원(QoQ +15.3%), 영업이익 1,130억원
(QoQ 흑전, AMPC 2,410억원 반영), OPM 1.5%(QoQ +4.7%p)로 영업이익 기준 컨
센서스(매출액 7.2조원, 영업이익 1,880억원)를 하회했다. 컨센서스 대비 영업이익이 
750억원 하회한 배경으로는 ESS 매출이 가이던스인 +40% 보다 하회한 +31.6%로 
부진한 영향이다. ESS 생산량이 예상 대비 부진한 이유는 셀 생산은 원활했으나 팩, 
컨테이너 CAPA가 병목으로 작용한 영향이다. ESS는 부진했으나 EV는 유럽을 중심
으로 VW, 르노 등 중저가 전기차 판매가 확대되었으며, T사와 R사향의 소형전지 판
매 호조로 매출액은 컨센서스에 부합했다. 
26년 3분기, ESS QoQ +42%로 ESS가 견인할 분기 
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26 매출액 9조 470억원(QoQ +19.7%), 영업이익 2,930억원
(QoQ 160,0% AMPC 3,860억원 반영), OPM 3.2%(QoQ +1.7%p)로 전망한다. ESS 
연간 신규 수주 목표는 90GWh로 상반기 ESS 신규 수주가 약 20GWh 수준임을 고
려시 하반기 70GWh의 신규 수주가 기대된다. 2분기부터 랜싱과 UC2가 양산이 시
작되며 동사의 글로벌 ESS CAPA 는 68GWh(중국 7GWh, 한국 1GWh, 미국 
57GWh, 폴란드 3GWh)이며 미국 CAPA 57GWh는 홀란드 17GWh, STL 10GWh, 
Honda 10GWh, 랜싱 10GWh, UC2 10GWh로 구분된다. 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 560,000원 유지한다. 
LG에너지솔루션에 대한 투자의견은 매수, 목표주가는 560,000원으로 유지한다. 
2026년 가이던스는 매출액 Y oY +15~20% 수준, OPM은 +MS% 제시했으며 연 내 
충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 전망한다. 얼티엄 공장의 가동은 8월로 예정되어 있어  
EV 부문의 AMPC 수취가 3분기부터 본격적으로 시작될 것으로 예상한다. 
목표주가 (유지) 560,000원

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: frayed nerves
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells&Illiana Jain
10:44 July 17 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The Westpac–MI Consumer Sentiment Index rose 4.1% in July; but at 83.9, the index is still in the
bottom decile of historical outcomes, pointing to deep pessimism. Lower fuel prices have provided
some breathing room, contributing to a less-negative assessment of ‘family finances vs a year ago’

(+5.6%) and ‘family finances next 12mths’ (+13.4%). Consumers remain sensitive to developments in
the Middle East, however, last week’s re-escalation of the conflict resulting in a progressive

### Page 2

deterioration in daily responses while the survey was in the field.
Views on the economic outlook are weak and little changed, the one- and five-years ahead sub-
indexes lifting just 0.6% and 0.7% in the month; more encouragingly, concerns over the jobs outlook
have eased. Most consumers still expect mortgage rates to rise over the next year, though the share
has declined from 66% to 60%, with a rising proportion reporting uncertainty about where rates are

headed. Ultimately, the survey reported a reprieve from worst-case fears but is also evidence of the
scale of challenges ahead.
The latest NAB business survey echoed similar themes. Business confidence continued to retrace the
sharp falls seen at the onset of the Middle East conflict, rising +9pts to a less-pessimistic reading of –5.
Lower fuel prices contributed to slower growth in purchase costs and final prices, but the cumulative
impact of these pressures have weighted on capital expenditure plans and labour demand. Business

conditions remain subdued, holding at +3 for a third consecutive month having weakened earlier in
2026. This is consistent with sluggish activity growth as households and businesses approach the
outlook with caution.
Offshore, US inflation data was supportive of interest rates remaining on hold in coming months.
Headline prices fell 0.4% in June as energy prices responded to the Middle East ceasefire agreed mid-
month, pulling annual inflation down from 4.2%yr to 3.5%yr. Of greater significance for monetary policy,

however, was the flat monthly read for core inflation, 2.6%yr.
Members of the FOMC want to see further evidence of disinflation towards the 2.0%yr target. In our
view, the multi-month trends are consistent with such an out-turn, with core goods prices broadly
unchanged year-to-date and core services inflation averaging just below 0.3% per month. The June
PPI report corroborates this view, headline producer prices falling 0.3%, while May was revised down

sharply from 1.1% to 0.6%. Ex food and energy, prices rose a modest 0.2%mth, and the prior month's
gain was revised lower from 0.4% to 0.1%. The latest readings on consumer demand are also
consistent with inflation returning to target, nominal control group retail sales up a modest 0.5% in June
after a 0.8% gain in May, and the latest Beige Book also pointing to consumer demand being
constrained by cost-of-living pressures. The duration and severity of the renewed conflict in the Middle

East will be critical to the US growth outlook given University of Michigan consumer sentiment remains
stuck near multi-decade lows.
Over in Asia, China's Q2 GDP and June activity data provided a mixed read on their economy. Q2 GDP
met expectations at 0.9%qtr, although the annual rate disappointed at 4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth
remains broadly consistent with our expectation of 4.7%, but such an outcome would be at the lower
end of authorities' 4.5–5.0% full-year target. The industrial sector continues to drive aggregate

momentum, production growth rebounding to 5.3%yr in June from around 4.0%yr through April and
May. But the domestic growth story remains fragile, primarily owing to declining investment, -5.7%ytd in
June. Retail sales growth recovered from -0.6%yr in May to 1.0%yr in June, though that still leaves it at
the lower end of the historical rage. Together, the state of domestic investment and household
consumption call for urgent pro-active stimulus to support cyclical momentum as well as structural

change to help the gains of trade flow to the wider economy.

### Page 3

Related articles
The Bank of Korea raised rates by 25bp this week and signalled further tightening is likely. Inflation
remains elevated, due to a combination of higher energy, exchange rate and food costs alongside
resilient domestic demand. But the BoK's greater challenge is managing an increasingly two-speed
economy, with tech-related sectors benefiting directly from the AI and data centre investment boom
while others wait for those gains to spill over. For now, policymakers appear comfortable leaning

against inflation and expecting the benefits of AI production and investment to broaden; although, in the
interim, this leaves Korea exposed should enthusiasm around AI turn. Investment and the implications
for growth and policy is a focus of this month's edition of The Wrap on Asia; and, with a particular focus
on AI investment, Chief Economist Luci Ellis’ weekly essay.

This special issue concerns the update of Eurozone data.
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting surge in oil and
gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their negative effects on growth be the
same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war in the Middle

East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and emerging countries, and to see
how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as necessary.
The expectation that the surge in inflation, driven by this new energy shock, would be more moderate than in 2022
(with demand being less dynamic and supply less constrained) is confirmed. However, following the Memorandum of

Understanding signed in mid-June between the United States and Iran, inflationary risk has eased but has not
disappeared. This MoU has reduced the risk of a severe escalation of the conflict, but the resurgence of tensions and
military strikes in mid-July shows that the situation is far from resolved.
When we compare the impact on economic activity of the current energy shock with that of 2022 (following the conflict
in Ukraine), the favorable point in 2026, for the euro area, is the business climate in the manufacturing sector, which is

holding up better than in 2022. Consumer confidence has fallen sharply but to a lesser extent in 2026 than in 2022. As
for the deterioration in the business climate in the services sector, it was immediate in 2026, whereas it occurred with a
few months' delay in 2022.

We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; the “input prices” and “output prices" components of the

composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); the “suppliers’ delivery times” component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,
of inflationary pressures in the making); household confidence, as reflected in its “assessment of financial situation in the
next 12 months” component (in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these

indicators are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the
level of the indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The assessment of the June data is positive and reinforces the encouraging signals from May data. Symmetrically to its rise,
which was largely driven by the "energy" component, inflation has significantly receded due to the marked drop in oil prices
in June. According to PMI business climate surveys, inflationary pressures have also eased. Delivery times are also benefiting

from the supportive impact of the MoU (signed in mid-June between the United States and Iran) and are improving slightly.
The business climate in the manufacturing sector has slipped again but marginally. Business confidence in the services
sector and consumer confidence continue to recover. The expected improvement due to the reduced risk of a severe
escalation of the conflict in Iran is materializing. It remains to be seen whether it will withstand the persistence of

uncertainties and the resurgence of tensions in mid-July.

Document 300: Food For Thought_ Sowing The Seeds of Food Inflation (2026-07-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Food For Thought_ Sowing The Seeds of Food Inflation (2026-07-15).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-07-15
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Food For Thought: Sowing 
The Seeds of Food Inflation

1 
Authors 
Maui Brennan 
Economist

Sanjay Raja 
Chief UK Economist

Maria Contreras 
Economist

July 15, 2026 
Ongoing tensions in the Middle East have raised the prospect of indirect effects 
on inflation - particularly in relation to food prices. Indeed, much has been said 
about the rise in energy prices and its spill over into other commodities such as 
fertiliser, CO2, and other key ingredients that sit within the agricultural pipeline. 
To be sure, both the size and duration of the shock matter for food prices. 
In this note, we do four things. 
— First, we highlight what’s happened to key commodity prices in the wake of 
the Iran shock. 
— Second, we set out a framework, estimating the potential impact of 
commodity price moves on food inflation for both the euro area and the UK. 
— Third, we stress test our estimates by looking at the duration of the shock 
and whether pass-through of cost increases change when the shock is 
deemed 'transitory' or 'persistent' . 
— Finally, we highlight some of the broader implications for inflation and where 
risks lie around our estimates. 
Our analysis points to renewed upside risks to food prices stemming from recent 
oil and fertiliser market developments. The effects appear larger in the UK than in 
the Euro area. 
Importantly, we find that pass-through isn't immediate, and in fact, can build 
over a long period of time given the mapping of commodity prices into food retail 
prices. What also matters is whether shocks prove persistent or transitory. 
Notwithstanding the MoU agreed to by the US and Iran, we estimate that the net 
effects from oil and urea prices will lead to modest inflationary pressure in the 
quarters ahead – something we will be watching for closely. That said, 
considerable uncertainty surrounds both the scale and timing of these effects, 
with a range of other supply-side factors also likely to influence outcomes. We 
will be monitoring market developments closely and will calibrate our 
projections accordingly.

### Page 2

Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
A recap on recent price movements
Recent geopolitical developments have been associated with large swings in
fertiliser and oil prices. Oil prices initially rose as markets priced supply-disruption
concerns. Subsequently, however, price rises retraced meaningfully as these
concerns eased with continuing peace deal negotiations. To add context to these
movements, we compare spot and futures prices before the episode, after the initial

Iran shock, and using the latest available market data. For the futures curves, we fit
a Nielsen-Siegel curve to trace out the shape of the curve across contract positions.
For fertiliser prices, we use the World Bank urea price series as a proxy for global
nitrogen fertiliser costs. Urea is one of the most widely traded nitrogen fertilisers
and is closely linked to the production process for agricultural inputs. We note,
however, that this captures only one important fertiliser-cost channel rather than

the complete range of farm input costs.
Figure 1: Brent futures pricing downward sloping...
Source : Deutsche Bank, Bloomberg Finance LP
Figure 2: …same as urea futures pricing
Source : Deutsche Bank, Bloomberg Finance LP
Before the Iran War, the spot price for Brent crude oil was trading at about USD
72.50/barrel. This then spiked to USD 118/barrel during the peak of the Iran/US
conflict, before settling at around USD 83/barrel at current market prices (figure 1).

Interestingly, the futures curve is currently flatter than the steeper curve observed
at the start of the war, which could imply a more gradual decline in prices in the next
few months. The urea spot price also increased sharply at the beginning of the Iran
war. In contrast, these prices have settled at a much lower level (figure 2).
Perhaps unsurprisingly, in terms of recent food price developments, we have yet to
see any tangible impact on retail prices.

Euro area food inflation has softened more than expected this year, declining from
2.5% y-o-y in Dec-25 to 1.6% in Jun-26 based on the flash estimate. This marks the
lowest reading for food HICP since mid-2021, with the bulk of the adjustment
occurring over the past two months. Focusing on food stuff, the largest
disinflationary impulses have come from dairy products, sugar and sweets, and
coffee. In 2025, these categories were among the main drivers of food inflation,

accounting for roughly 35% of annual food inflation despite representing only 18%
of the HICP food basket. In recent months, their contribution has fallen to just over
20%. Meat prices have also eased somewhat from their 2025 peak, although they
remain the largest contributor to food inflation.
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
In the UK, we see a similar story. Heavy discounting has led to a drop-off in food
prices. Food and non-alcoholic beverages CPI slowed to 2.2% y-o-y – reaching its
lowest rate in 17 months. The slowdown in food prices can be seen across both
processed and unprocessed food. The latest PPI data for food points to further
disinflation in the pipeline, with producer prices across food, beverages and
tobacco slowing to 1.1% y-o-y in May-26.

Figure 3: Food (and non-alcoholic beverages) inflation in the EA and the UK has
been on a downtrend this year
Source : Deutsche Bank, Macrobond
How does energy and fertiliser costs pass-through into food inflation? There are
two channels in which oil and fertiliser prices work their way into CPI.
Channel 1: the direct energy cost. Oil enters food at pretty much every node in the
food chain. For example, manufacturers need diesel for tractors and irrigation

pumps, marine freight and trucking, processing heat, packaging (plastics) and
refrigeration. Energy-related inputs account for around 5–10% of farm production
costs, although the share can be significantly higher in energy-intensive sectors
such as horticulture and poultry.
Channel 2: fertiliser cost. Urea is essentially manufactured from natural gas. The
higher gas prices go, the higher fertiliser prices go too. The response can effectively
be summarised into two segments: an immediate margin squeeze, then a supply

response as farmers cut application rates or switch acreage to lower Nitrogen crops
(soybeans over maize), which lifts prices with a lag of a full crop cycle.
The transmission from input costs to retail prices, as a result, is heavily staggered.
The upstream leg is fast: ammonia and urea reprice off gas within weeks. Put
simply, an energy shock is visible in fertiliser markets almost immediately. The
middle leg is slow and seasonal — application decisions are made pre-season, so

a nitrogen cut driven by a spring price spike doesn't show up until (as early) as the
following harvest. Ultimately, the final leg is both lagged and heavily damped.
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 4

Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Estimating pass-through to food prices
There are several ways to estimate the effect of oil and fertilizer price shocks to food
inflation. In this case, we opt for a simple approach that allows the "data to speak":
the local projection method (Jorda, 2005). Essentially what we do is trace out the
impact of a shock over time, in this case oil and fertilizer price shocks, by running
a separate regression for each forecast horizon (1-month ahead, 2-months ahead

and so on...).
A key challenge is identifying the commodity-price shock itself. Oil and fertiliser
prices move for many different reasons, including global demand, exchange rates,
trade conditions, inflationary pressures and supply disruptions. To reduce the risk
of conflating commodity-specific surprises with broader macroeconomic
developments, we use a Romer and Romer-style approach (Romer & Romer, 2004).
The above approach involves regressing the shocks of interest on a set of potentially

confounding variables, including the USD effective exchange rate, monthly dummy
variables to capture seasonality, lags of the shocks of interest to account for
persistence, global trade volumes and global CPI inflation. We then extract the
residuals from this regression. These residuals could then be interpreted as
movements in oil and urea prices that are not explained by our macroeconomic
controls.
The shocks of interest that we use are the month-on-month percentage changes in

Brent crude oil spot prices and the World Bank urea commodity price series.
For the local projections, we use the HICP Food & Non-alcoholic beverages series
for the Euro area and the UK Consumer Price Index Food and Non-alcoholic
beverages. The regressions include the shock of interest (oil & urea), and three lags
of the price variables. We then estimate the cumulative percent impact on the price
level from a percentage point surprise in both oil and urea costs.

Baseline results
The baseline estimates suggest that oil and fertiliser price surprises have a
measurable effect on food prices. The estimated response varies across the two
economies and commodities, with the UK showing larger pass-through than the
euro area in our specifications. Our estimates, however, become relatively more
uncertain the further out we go as shown by the widening error bands.
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Figure 4: A 1pp shock in oil price leads to nearly a 7bps
increase in food HICP (EA)...
Source :Deutsche Bank
Figure 5:...and just over 10bps in the UK (CPI)
Source :Deutsche Bank
Figure 6: A 1pp shock to urea prices results in just over
4bps to EA HICP (food & non-alcoholic beverages)...
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 7:...and close to 6bps for UK CPI (food and non-
alcoholic beverages)
Source : Deutsche Bank
In further detail, figures 4-7 present the impact of a 1 percentage point increase in

monthly oil and urea price growth on the cumulative food & non-alcoholic
beverages price level. Looking first at fertiliser price shocks, we find that the peak
impact on UK food prices occurs after around 20 months with a 0.07% cumulative
impact on the food price level (figure 7). In the Euro area, the peak impact is slightly
lower at around 0.05% and also occurs after roughly 20 months (figure 6). A broadly
similar pattern is visible for oil price shocks. The UK experiences a larger cumulative

increase of 0.1% in food prices while the Euro area sees an impact of about 0.07%.
There are a few possible explanations for the slightly larger estimated effects in the
UK relative to the Euro area. One possible explanation that may be driving the
difference in pass-through between the Euro area and the UK is the higher imported
share in the UK food basket. This would make importers and producers more
exposed to externally driven input cost pressures.

A second explanation may come from sterling weakness during global energy
shocks.
A third explanation may be in fact that many UK farming sectors operate on narrow
margins. When energy, fertiliser or feed costs rise sharply, farmers have less ability
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 6

Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
to absorb the shock and are more likely to pass it on through the supply chain.
Defra's Farm Business Survey consistently shows that profitability is highly
sensitive to movements in input costs. UK agriculture is also relatively intensive,
particularly in cereals, oilseeds and grassland systems.
The impact of the Iran war
So far, the results have shown a 1 percentage point increase in fertiliser and oil price
inflation. However, events such as the Iran war have led to big swings in both urea

and oil prices – and in both directions. In order to better gauge these impacts, we
scale the shocks in the baseline results to the actual movements observed from
March to June. The figures below show the estimated cumulative food price pass-
through implied by each monthly commodity price move.
Figure 8: Estimated urea shock pass-through for each
month since the conflict began in the EA...
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 9:...and the UK
Source : Deutsche Bank

Figure 10: Scaled oil shock between March and June for
the EA...
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 11: ...and the UK
Source : Deutsche Bank
To be sure, the March shock impact alone would have had a big impact on food
prices in the Euro area and the UK. For both oil and urea, it was an upside surprise
of over 40 percentage points (over 3 standard deviations). However, over the course
of the past few months, there have been significant reversals in both oil and urea

prices. We attempt to capture these offsetting shocks and obtain the overall impact
since March. How do we do this? We do this by aggregating the impacts of the
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
monthly urea and oil price shocks – i.e. we aggregate the shocks from oil and urea
via superposition of the estimated local projections.
Figure 12: Estimated aggregate urea shock on food HICP
in the EA...
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 13:...and on UK food CPI
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 14: Estimated aggregated oil shock on food HICP in
the EA…
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 15:...and on UK food CPI
Source : Deutsche Bank

We trace out these shocks and find some interesting implications for the overall
impact. For the UK, we see a cumulative impact of around 1% on food prices from
the oil shocks a year from March and 0.3% from a urea shock peaking also in a year's
time from March, i.e. totalling nearly 130bps (though the effect starts to unwind
four quarters out). For the Euro area, the oil shocks lead to about a 0.5% cumulative
increase in a year from March and about a 0.3% impact from urea shocks in a year

from March. Overall, we observe similar patterns where the UK experiences a
slightly stronger impact from the recent shocks compared to the Euro area.
Stress testing our results: persistent vs transitory shocks
A simple scaling of the shocks is unlikely to fully reflect firms’ pricing behaviour. In
practice, firms may respond differently to the size and duration of the shock. A large
but temporary increase in oil or fertiliser prices may generate less pass-through

than a smaller but more sustained rise.
To capture this distinction, we split our oil and fertiliser shocks between persistent
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 8

Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
and transitory. We classify a shock as persistent if the price remains at least half as
large after four months. If the price falls by more than half over that period, we treat
it as transitory. We only apply this classification to sizeable shocks, defined as
moves larger than one standard deviation in the relevant shock series. This gives us
a simple way to separate temporary price spikes from more sustained input-cost
pressures.

We then run local projections again on the persistent and short-lived shocks and we
obtain the following results:
Figure 16: Persistent urea price shock...
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 17:...vs transitory urea price shock
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 18: Persistent oil price shock...
Source : Deutsche Bank
Figure 19: ...vs transitory oil price shock
Source : Deutsche Bank
The results suggest that persistence matters, particularly for oil shocks (figures 18

and 19). When the shock is persistent, we find a stronger peak impact for both the
Euro area and the United Kingdom  for a given 1 percentage point surprise
compared to a short-lived shock. For urea, the pattern is clearer in the UK, where
persistent shocks are associated with a larger food price pass-through. In the Euro
area, however, the difference between persistent and short-lived shocks is less
clear. Overall, the evidence points to a stronger role for shock persistence in the UK,

while the Euro area results are more mixed.
While it is too early to definitely classify the latest energy price shocks, the above
results highlight an important distinction. If recent recent rises in oil and fertiliser
prices prove short-lived and unwind over the coming months, then pass-through to
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 9

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
food prices may be more limited. However, if prices remain elevated, history
suggests we could expect a larger and more sustained impact on food inflation.
Based on the shocks observed so far, our classification leans towards the recent
developments being transitory in nature. That said, the classification is far from
certain and will depend on how oil and fertiliser prices evolve through July and the
months ahead.
Conclusions

What to make from the above analyses? We highlight several key findings.
First, the impact of a fertiliser and oil price shock and its pass-through to food price
levels tends to be stronger in the United Kingdom than in the Euro area.
Second, after scaling and aggregating the fertiliser and oil price shocks from March
up to June, we find that in a year's time from March, the cumulative impact on the
UK food price level could be about 1.3% while for the Euro area it would be around

0.8% instead – implying a 0.1-0.15pp increase in headline HICP/CPI.
Third, we also recognise the limitations of simply scaling these shocks. The size and
duration of the shock can result in very different implications for food price pass-
through. The persistence of a shock can have a much larger impact, especially in the
UK. It's too early to say where the current energy shock will fall into. Recent price
action suggests a further wave of energy pressures on the back of escalating Middle

East tensions. We will be monitoring this closely. The longer commodity prices
remain elevated, the more they feed through into food prices.
Lastly, the confidence bands around our estimates suggest wide variation in pass-
through. Given the swings in price rises across oil and urea, the staggered pass-
through of cost rises into the food supply chain may be more varied. While our
estimates provide a measure of the average conditional pass-through, other factors

will also impact pass-through. For example, warmer global temperatures, less
rainfall, and changing labour costs alongside ongoing geopolitical tensions may
also shape pricing strategies.
To be sure, despite the pull back in oil and urea prices from their peak, the net effects
still point to a modest jump in food (and non-alcoholic beverages) prices.
Furthermore, the recent jump back in price momentum will do little to assuage
concerns of indirect effects brewing within inflation. We will reflect some of our

findings over the coming week(s) as we update our inflation projections.
15 July 2026
Focus Europe

营收 608 亿创新⾼， Meta 盘后却⼤跌。刺痛市场的是巨额 “AI 账单 ” ：资本开⽀狂飙致⾃由现
⾦流暴跌 91% ，转负在即。不同于微软卖算⼒， Meta ⽤ AI“ 挖矿 ” 变现太慢。当收⼊增速跑
不过烧钱速度，资本正对这场深不⻅底的重资产转型失去耐⼼。
摘要：
Meta ⼆季度营收 608 亿美元，创历史新⾼，同⽐增⻓ 28% 。盘后跌 7% 。
同⼀天发财报的微软，云业务超预期，盘后涨超 8% 。 Meta 的营收也超越了华尔街⼀致预期，⼴

息。
但市场和 Meta 之间，正在发⽣⼀场关于 "AI 账单由谁来付 " 的分歧。把财报翻到现⾦流那⼀⻚，
画⻛骤变：单季经营现⾦流 318.6 亿美元，扣掉 310.8 亿美元的资本开⽀，⾃由现⾦流只剩 7.84
亿美元。去年同期这个数字是 85.5 亿。⼀年之内，蒸发了 91% 。
如果保持这个烧钱速度，下个季度⾃由现⾦流转负⼏乎没有悬念。⾕歌在上周的财报⾥已经率

先进⼊了负现⾦流区间 —— 那是⾕歌历史上第⼀次。
这不是业绩暴雷，是 AI 军备竞赛的账单到了。
" ⼀次性费⽤ " 遮不住结构问题
CFO 苏珊 · 李在电话会上拆解了利润下滑的原因：⼆季度总费⽤ 420 亿美元，同⽐增 55% ，其中
包含 24 亿美元的法律诉讼拨备和 11.8 亿美元的裁员遣散费。剔除这两项，营业利润本该同⽐增
⻓约 9% 。
这是⼀个说得过去的解释。但也仅⽌于此。
真正值得注意的数字藏在费⽤结构⾥：研发⽀出单季 216.6 亿美元，同⽐激增 67% ，占营收的⽐
例从去年同期的 27% 跳到 36% 。这笔钱不是诉讼拨备，不是遣散费 —— 它是 Meta 为 AI 交的⽉
供，⽽且每个⽉都在涨。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

资本开⽀从去年同期的 170.1 亿飙到 310.8 亿，增幅 83% 。公司把全年 capex 指引从 1250-1450 亿
收窄⾄ 1300-1450 亿，看起来是收敛，实际上是下限从 1250 抬到 1300 ，上限没动。换⼀种读
法：⾄少 1300 亿美元的年度 AI 投⼊底线已经锁定了。
AI 在⼴告端确实有⽤，但跑不过账单
扎克伯格在电话会上花了很⻓篇幅讲 AI 落地： Advantage+ AI ⼴告⼯具年化收⼊ run rate 超过 750
亿美元， 900 万⼴告主在⽤⾄少⼀种 Meta AI ⼯具， LLM 正在被整合进推荐系统， Instagram ⽤⼾
时⻓双位数增⻓。
这些是真实进展，不是画饼。但⼴告业务的增速跑不过资本开⽀的增速 —— 收⼊同⽐涨 27% ，
capex 同⽐涨 83% ，三⽐⼀的差距。算上研发费⽤， AI 相关总投⼊的增速更快。与此同时收⼊增
速在放缓： Q1 是 33% ， Q2 降到 28% ， Q3 指引中值 625 亿美元，对应同⽐约 22%—— 虽然依然是

两位数增⻓，但减速趋势明确。
利润率也在收缩。运营利润率从去年同期的 43% 降到 31% 。 AI ⼴告⼯具确实在改善转化率、提
升⼴告单价，但同时也推⾼了推理成本。 Meta 跟微软、⾕歌不⼀样，它没有成熟的云业务把算
⼒冗余直接变现。微软有 Azure ，⾕歌有 Google Cloud ， Meta 什么都没有。
扎克伯格在电话会上拒绝了 " 卖算⼒换利润 " 的路⼦。他说收到的算⼒租赁报价远⾼于成本价，但
仅仅卖掉所有算⼒获取短期利润是愚蠢的。他赌的是另⼀条路 ——" 卖智能 " 的⻓期利润率会远⾼
于 " 卖算⼒ " 。逻辑上说得通。但市场在问的是另⼀件事：在 " 卖智能 " 真正规模化之前，⾃由现⾦
流从 85 亿跌到不⾜ 8 亿、下半年可能转负的账，谁来扛？
同⼀天发财报，两种叙事的分岔⼝
微软和 Meta 同⼀天发财报，反应截然相反。
微软的 Azure 连续增⻓加速，说明 AI 云服务有真实的、可测量的、按需付费的商业循环，企业对
AI 算⼒的需求直接转化为云收⼊，链条短⽽清晰。

Meta 的闭环要⻓得多 ——AI 改善⼴告推荐，⼴告主看到更好的 ROI ，增加投放，⼴告收⼊增
⻓。链条上每⼀环都真实存在，但环节越多，从投⼊到回报的周期越⻓，不确定性越⼤。
说得更直⽩⼀点：微软在卖铲⼦， Meta 在⽤铲⼦挖⾃⼰的矿。
卖铲⼦收账快；挖矿得先投⼊，回报看矿的品位和矿价。⼴告就是 Meta 的矿，矿本⾝不错 ——
⼆季度⼴告单价涨了 12% ，展⽰量涨了 14% ， Instagram ⽇活突破 20 亿。但挖矿成本正以三倍于
矿价涨幅的速度往上⾛。
36 亿⽇活：最深的护城河，也是最重的包袱
Meta 应⽤家族⽇活 36 亿，同⽐增⻓ 3% 。 Instagram ⽇活刚突破 20 亿， Threads ⽉活超 5 亿，
WhatsApp 在世界杯决赛期间峰值每秒 3000 万条消息。
这个⽤⼾基数意味着 Meta 拥有全球最⼤的 AI 分发和测试场景。推荐算法、⼴告投放、内容⽣
成，随便哪个⽅向， Meta 都有最海量、最多样化的训练数据和反馈回路。
但硬币的另⼀⾯是， 36 亿⽤⼾对 AI 推理的需求也是 36 亿⼈体量的。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
74 收藏 分享到 :
每⼀条推荐的个性化⽣成、每⼀次⼴告的实时竞价优化、每⼀个⽤⼾⾏为的特征提取，背后都
在烧推理算⼒。收⼊在涨，推理成本涨得更快。在 Meta ⾝上，这表现为⼴告效率的提升追不上
AI 基础设施账单的增速。
市场到底在担⼼什么
盘后跌 7% 不是对⼀个季度 EPS miss 的惩罚，是对⼀个核⼼假设的动摇。市场此前默认 Meta 的
AI 投资可以在 12 到 18 个⽉内被收⼊增速覆盖，从⽽维持或改善利润率。
Q2 给的答案是反⽅向的：收⼊增速在放缓（ 28% 往 22% ⾛）， capex 在加速（ 83% ），利润率在
压缩（ 43% 到 31% ），⾃由现⾦流接近归零。
扎克伯格的叙事框架 ——AI 加速核⼼业务、下⼀代个⼈智能体、企业级市场 —— 是完整的，逻辑
上也⾃洽。但市场要的已经不是叙事，是数字。具体说，是在某个时间点，收⼊增速开始反超

成本增速的那个拐点。
Q2 财报没有给出这个拐点。 Q3 指引也没有。苏珊 · 李说 2026-2027 年是 " 全⼒扩产 " 阶段， 2028 年
后才开始 " 预留调整空间 " 。翻译过来就是：⾄少还有 18 个⽉的⾼强度投⼊。
Q3 是⼀个⾮常关键的检验窗⼝。⾃由现⾦流只剩 7.84 亿， capex 只增不减，转负⼏乎是时间问
题 —— 到那时候，市场怎么读这个 " 负号 " ，会决定股价的下⼀段⽅向。
⼴告收⼊的减速趋势能不能⽌住同样重要， Q3 指引中值 625 亿美元对应同⽐约 22% ，如果实际
能贴近上限 25% ，说明变现效率在加速；落在下限甚⾄ miss ，压⼒就不是跌⼀天的事了。
扎克伯格嘴上说 " 不卖算⼒换利润 " ，但在电话会上也给⾃⼰留了退路 —— ⾯向企业提供 API 和智
能体， " 未来还可能直接对外出售算⼒以及其他配套服务 " 。如果他真开始动了这块蛋糕，哪怕规
模不⼤，对市场来说就是⼀条新故事线。
但⽐这些更根本的问题是，他在 "AI 投⼊何时开始收敛 " 上始终没有给出时间线。在⾃由现⾦流即

将转负的节点上，市场给⻓期愿景的估值溢价正在消退，耐⼼是⼀种奢侈品。
Meta 正在讲⼀个巨型科技公司历史上最⼤胆的转型故事：从轻资产的⼴告平台，变成重资产的
AI 基础设施运营商加智能服务商。营收 608 亿、⽇活 36 亿的底⼦让这个故事有顶级的基本⾯⽀
撑。
但 310.8 亿美元的单季资本开⽀，以及由此带来的 91% ⾃由现⾦流崩塌，也在告诉市场同⼀件
事：这笔转型费⽐所有⼈预想的都贵，⽽且账单才翻到第⼆⻚。

Now for the hard part 
• Oil, elevated real bond yields and central bank policy dominate the near-term 
market narrative . We remain constructive on risky assets, given supportive 
growth and earnings fundamentals, but the ride is expected to be bumpy.  
• Strong earnings growth to underpin global equities; stay Overweight. Our 
regional preferences for the US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) markets remain in place, 
though we seek to broaden sector exposure by upgrading the US financial sector. 
• High real bond yields argue for locking in income; raise bonds to a Core 
holding, preferring Emerging Market (EM) USD bonds.  Gold is reduced to a 
Core holding as elevated bond yields are a headwind for a speedy price recovery. 
Energy prices, yields and central banks dominate the narrative 
H2 2026 has started on a muted note. Major equity markets, gold and the USD have 
held within a relatively tight range. AI and semiconductor equities have faced relatively 
greater headwinds as US bond yields and oil prices have moved higher.  
Of the pivot points we laid out in our H2 2026 Outlook – energy prices, equity supply, 
investor positioning and central bank policy – energy prices and central bank policy 
dominate the narrative. Renewed Middle East conflict escalation resulted in oil prices 
rising briefly above USD 90/bbl, reigniting concerns over its inflationary impact. 
However, we continue to hold the view that prices will remain largely rangebound 
within USD 70-90/bbl, with any short-lived moves above this range likely to incentivise 
conflict de-escalation to avoid a bigger inflation shock and a hawkish shift by the Fed. 
A more notable development in recent months has been the rise in real (net -of-
inflation) bond yields . Since mid -May, the real 10 -year US bond yield has risen 
towards 2.5% from below 2%. An extension of this move above 2.5% (the 2023 peak) 
would result in the highest real yields since 2008, potentially triggering a negative 
reaction in equities. Growing Fed policy uncertainty under Chair Warsh and persistent 
concerns over Developed Market (DM) debt levels are likely behind this move. 
However, we believe today’s ‘soft landing’ environment will extend  as the Fed 
avoids actual rate hikes and caps real yields. Inflation remains a risk worth monitoring, 
but contained oil prices and inflation expectations remain encouraging.

Navigating a bumpy equity market 
We remain Overweight global equities relative to bonds 
and cash.  However, as we laid out in our H2 outlook, the 
near-term path is likely to be a relatively bumpy one. 
The trade -offs are best captured via our quantitative 
stock-bond model. While it remains Overweight equities, the 
magnitude of optimism has reduced. Fundamentals now 
remain the key support for stocks, with technicals and 
valuations turning less supportive.  This matches the wider 
narrative of strong equity earnings growth fundamentals 
balancing against higher real yields and short -term worries 
focused on energy prices, central bank policy and AI.  
On balance, we expect optimism to ultimately persist , 
given strong earnings fundamentals, albeit with a higher level 
of volatility. This is why we retain our regional Overweight 
views on the US and AxJ in Foundation portfolios  and would 
consider adding to our preferred markets on pullbacks.  
Broadening sector exposure via opportunistic 
allocations is also key to navigating the current 
environment. We upgrade US financials to Overweight, 
which leaves us with a preference for the sector across the 
US, Euro area and Japan equity markets. Higher bond yields 
remain a key driver of net interest margins (NIMs) across most 
regions. However, an active M&A and  initial public offering 
(IPO) environment are also important, particularly in the US. 
Meanwhile, we retain our US communication services sector 
Opportunistic idea, with the strength in earnings growth 
expected to outweigh lingering doubts over the pace of AI 
capital expenditure (capex) monetisation.  
Looking further into H2 2026, the November US mid-term 
election remains a volatility risk.  Historically, since 1990, 
the S&P500 has faced an average drawdown of around 16% 
during US mid-term election years, with a range spanning 7.3-
33%. The fact that the S&P 500 already faced a 9.4% 
drawdown from late January to early March means we are 
reluctant to forecast another similar episode in the coming 
months. However, this historical view argues for ensuring 
portfolios are prepared for higher-than-usual volatility. 
Locking in (real) yields 
We take a glass-half-full approach to bonds, raising them 
to a Core holding.  While we acknowledge that rising real 
bond yields are a potential risk worth monitoring, we expect 
the lack of follow-through on actual rate hikes by most major 
central banks to ultimately defuse real bond yields. This 
argues for using the rise in bond yi elds to lock in attractive 
(real) yields.  
Within bonds, our preference for corporate and EM bonds 
over G3 government bonds remains unchanged. While the 
yield premium offered by the former remains relatively small 
by historical standards, we believe still -high credit quality 
continues to justify this elevated valuation.  
Our Overweight to EM USD bonds reflects both strong 
(fiscal) fundamentals and more attractive relative value.  
US Investment -grade (IG) corporate bonds may face 
temporary headwinds from significant hyperscaler debt 
supply, but this reflects a temporary supply influx rather than 
any deterioration in fundamental credit quality, in our view. 
Maintaining short duration in USD bond portfolios, 
however, is key to navigating these opportunities and 
risks. The rise in 10-year bond yields, both nominal and real, 
reinforces the risk of elevated volatility from excessively long 
maturity bonds. We do not believe the gap between short and 
long maturity bonds currently compensates investors 
adequately for the risk and, thus, prefer short (3 -5 years) 
duration in USD bonds. 
Reduce gold to a Core holding 
We reduce gold to a Core holding. While prices have shown 
signs of stability in recent weeks, higher bond yields are 
setting a higher bar to beat. We remain positive on gold prices 
in absolute terms – now expecting USD 4,600/oz over the next 
12 months – and thus see current levels as attractive to 
accumulate in under-allocated portfolios. However, we expect 
the pace of the price rebound to be relatively shallow, given 
elevated US bond yields.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary – A changing world demands a new investment discipline

Asset allocation has undergone a profound structural transformation. Public and private markets are converging, inflation 
and interest rate regimes have shifted in ways that challenge long -held assumptions about diversification, and the structural 
forces shaping returns (debt, demographics, geopolitical fragmentation and artificial intelligence) now cut across traditional asset 
class boundaries in ways that a siloed investment process is not designed to address.  
Against this backdrop, the conventional approach to portfolio construction – allocating fixed percentages to Equities, Fixed 
Income and Alternatives, each managed independently against its own benchmark – is showing its limitations. The year 2022 
served as a stark reminder that stock-bond diversification can fail, as both asset classes declined simultaneously, exposing 
the fragility of a framework built on the long-held assumption that these two asset classes provide mutual protection. 
To address these vulnerabilities, the Total Portfolio Approach (TPA) is emerging as a guiding framework for portfolio construction. 
It reflects a fundamental shift in how asset allocators think about risk, return and the purpose of each investment within the 
portfolio. According to research by the CAIA Association and the Thinking Ahead Institute, institutions that have formally adopted 
TPA have outperformed their Strategic Asset Allocation (SAA) peers by an average of 1.3% per year  – a meaningful and 
compounding advantage over time.

Key findings

Beyond asset class labels: What TPA means 
At its core, TPA is a holistic portfolio strategy that breaks down 
siloed thinking, aligns all assets to a unified purpose and 
empowers investors to allocate capital dynamically across 
market environments.  
Rather than organising the portfolio as a collection of discrete 
asset class buckets, TPA treats the portfolio as a  single, 
integrated system. Every allocation decision is assessed in 
terms of its marginal contribution to total return, total risk and 
the interaction of exposures across the whole portfolio.  
The underlying logic is directly applicable to wealth portfolios 
of meaningful scale and complexity. The framework rests on 
four interconnected dimensions:  
• Empowered governance:  Replaces rigid, siloed asset -
class caps with a governance model that gives an 
investment team the strategic flexibility to allocate capital 
based on total-portfolio objectives; 
• A single portfolio culture, in which all stakeholders are 
aligned with the objectives of the total portfolio; 
• A ‘competition for capital’  mindset, under which every 
investment must justify its place by demonstrating it 
improves the portfolio's ability to meet its objectives; and  
• A factor-based lens that looks beyond asset class labels 
to focus on the underlying economic drivers of risk and 
return.  
Taken together, these dimensions shift portfolio management 
from a rules -based process toward s a judgment -based 
discipline.

TPA framework

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

TPA is not a rejection of SAA and can, in fact, be combined 
with it . The approach  integrates a way of asking what a 
portfolio is truly for and whether every position within it is 
genuinely earning its place. 
A factor-based lens: The hidden concentration 
risk most portfolios carry  
One of the most important insights that TPA brings to portfolio 
construction is the distinction between how a portfolio appears 
to be diversified and how it actually behaves under stress. 
Apparent diversification by asset class can mask significant 
concentration in a limited set of underlying economic drivers. 
When those drivers move together (as they did in 2022) 
sensitivity to a narrow range of outcomes can rise sharply, 
often in ways that surprise even experienced investors.  
The chart below (see Fig. 9) illustrates this point. What looks 
like a diversified portfolio – spanning public equity, private 
equity, government bonds, IG and private credit, real estate 
and infrastructure – largely resolves, at the factor level, into a 
single dominant exposure: Growth. When growth falters, 
these asset classes tend to fall together, regardless of how 
they are labelled in the portfolio. 
Fig. 9 Factor decomposition of a traditional multi-
asset portfolio 
Asset class allocation vs. risk factor allocation

Source: Standard Chartered 
TPA addresses this by using risk , rather than capital, as the 
primary driver of allocation, distributing the risk budget across 
genuinely distinct return drivers: the equity risk premium, the 
interest rate premium, inflation -sensitive exposures, 
alternative risk premia and pure idiosyncratic alpha. 
Our Balanced Portfolio through two lenses 
To illustrate how TPA changes the way we analyse and 
construct portfolios, we compare our Foundation+ Balanced 
Model Portfolio as viewed through two distinct lenses: its 
current Tactical Asset Allocation (TAA) and a TPA framework. 
Under the TAA framework, the portfolio is organised by asset 
class: Cash at 3%, Fixed Income at 30%, Equity at 43%, 
Gold at 4% and Alternatives at 20%.  This view is intuitive 
and familiar, but it tells us relatively little about the underlying 
risk drivers at work or how those exposures interact under 
different market conditions. 
Fig. 10 TPA applied to our Foundation+ Balanced TAA  
Risk-return allocation (in percentage terms)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Under the TPA framework, the same portfolio is reorganised 
by return stream and risk factor: 
• Market beta: The broad equity risk premium accounts for 
60%, reflecting the intentional dominance of growth -
oriented returns in our balanced mandate.  
• Active return streams (or alpha):  These represent 
targeted sources of return explicitly sized within the overall 
risk budget through Equity (4%), Fixed Income (1%) and 
Hedge Funds (3%). 
• Illiquid premia: These represent 12%, capturing the 
return premium available from private assets – a 
deliberate and compensated source of excess return.  
• Factor tilts: Account for 5%, introducing diversifying 
return streams, such as momentum, value and quality, 
which behave differently from plain market beta.  
• Inflation-sensitive assets : Represent 3%, providing a 
structural hedge against purchasing power erosion.  
The critical insight is not that the portfolio has changed 
dramatically in composition, but that the risk is now 
consciously and explicitly allocated rather than accidentally 
concentrated. It is this curatorial discipline, applied with rigour 
and consistency, that can serve investors' long-term interests 
most effectively in the investment environment ahead.  If you 
would like to talk about TPA and its implications for your 
portfolio, please contact your Investment Advisor or 
Relationship Manager.

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic ‘soft landing’ remains our base case , but we have lowered the 
probability from 60% in favour of a ‘no landing’. The global economy has withstood this year’s oil shock well. Business confidence 
picked up in June across major economies. The case for reflation has risen on the margin , given an acceleration in AI 
investments, the wealth effect from a booming stock market and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan. In this scenario, the Fed 
is likely to hold rates steady this year. The ECB is likely to deliver another ‘insurance’ hike in H2 2026, while the BoJ should hike 
once more this year to counter domestic inflation. China is likely to ease liquidity in H2 to revive domestic demand.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We raise the probability from 20%, expecting a de -escalation in the Middle East 
ahead of the US mid-term elections. If oil prices ease, US tax cuts, an AI-fuelled stock market boom and fiscal easing in Germany 
and Japan could boost ‘animal spirits’. A Russia-Ukraine peace deal or global defence spending boom can lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): This tail risk scenario incorporates 10% chance of a recession, potentially caused by delayed 
restart of Hormuz shipping, a stock market downturn hurting investor confidence or a bond sell-off due to inflation or debt fears. 
We also assign a 15% chance to a stagflation scenario if the Middle East conflict worsens and oil settles above USD 100/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
Fed to hold rates this year:  The US economy remains 
resilient, with recession risk low. Full -year 2026 GDP growth 
estimates cluster around 2.1-2.3%, moderating towards 2.1% 
in 2027, supported by resilient consumer spending and strong 
AI investment. The dominant tailwind is AI -related capital 
spending, which has lifted tech investment to record shares of 
GDP and is broadening into non -tech sectors. The key 
vulnerability is concentration: growth increasingly leans on AI 
capex and low household savings, leaving  business activity 
sensitive to any reversal in AI-related equity valuations. 
US inflation remains above target, but is showing early signs 
of easing. June core Personal Consumption Expenditures 
(PCE) inflation slowed to 0.1% m/m and 3.3% y/y  from 0.3% 
m/m and 3.4% y/y in May , with services inflation slowing 
sharply to 0.1% m/m . Core PCE inflation has run above 3%, 
hotter than core CPI, given tariff pass-through, tech/software 
prices and services. We expect inflation to settle close to 2% 
once the current energy shock fades. US wage growth, the 
primary driver of long-term inflation, remains on a downtrend, 
while forward indicators of shelter inflation, which accounts for 
one-third of US inflation, continues to cool. 
The Fed held its policy rate in July at 3.75%, although 
segments of the market were expecting a rate hike. We expect 
the Fed to hold its policy rate for the rest of the year as energy-
driven inflation gradually fades and the job market remains in 
balance. The Warsh-led Fed has sharpened its price-stability 
focus while cutting forward guidance , raising the prospect of 
bond volatility. Nevertheless, with oil likely rangebound (USD 
70-90/bbl), peak Fed hawkishness is probably behind us. 
We expect another ECB ‘insurance ’ hike: The Euro area 
entered 2026 with weak momentum, and the Middle East 
conflict weighed further on sentiment. A recent bounce in 
surveys, including a July ZEW survey beat and stronger 
German IFO expectations, was largely energy -driven and 
unlikely to last, wit h current conditions still depressed and

Global Market Outlook 10 
financial conditions having tightened again. Underlying 
activity is more resilient than headline data suggest s, with 
growth (ex-Ireland) around 0.3% q/q, though Germany faces 
a possible technical recession and export weakness remains 
structural. Full-year 2026 GDP growth forecasts cluster in a 
soft 0.3 -0.8% range, with a firmer recovery pencilled in for 
2027 and dependent on German fiscal spending picking up. 
Inflationary pressures remain muted outside energy. June 
inflation cooled to 2.8% y/y, with core inflation easing to 2.4%, 
suggesting core and services inflation have peaked. The key 
upside risk is a renewed energy shock – European natural gas 
prices have returned to March peaks. An early normalisation 
of Strait of Hormuz shipping (our base case) should lower 
inflation towards 2% by Q2 next year. Wage pressures stay 
contained and inflation expectations broadly anchored. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.25% while preserving 
hawkish optionality, warning the full energy -shock effects 
have yet to play out.  We expect one more 25bps ‘insurance’ 
hike this year, with timing dependent on energy prices, 
incoming inflation, and wage data. 
China to accelerate fiscal spending implementation as 
growth slows.  China's economic activity slowed in Q2 as 
domestic demand weakened further following a brief Q1 
recovery, with both investment and consumption losing 
momentum and the housing -market correction deepening. 
Still-buoyant exports, supported by the global AI bo om, have 
not been sufficient to offset the drag from soft domestic 
activity. Weakening credit and fiscal impulse signals softer 
growth ahead. With the property sector remaining a drag, 
export growth and expected acceleration in fiscal spending 
are likely to be key drivers of the economy in H2. 
Inflation is turning gradually higher. The reappearance of 
producer price pressures is likely one reason for the central 
bank to keep its policy rate on hold . This ‘return of inflation ’ 
marks a shift from the deflationary concerns that had 
dominated, and it constrains the scope for further monetary 
easing. 
Policymakers are unlikely to deliver broad -based stimulus, 
although targeted measure for priority sectors are likely. The 
Politburo pledged accelerated fiscal spending, including faster

execution of budgeted measures, mainly infrastructure 
spending on AI, the green transition and livelihood projects, 
while retaining a contingency plan. Monetary policy is likely to 
play a supplementary role: a 25bps bank reserve ratio cut is 
likely to keep liquidity ample, but the policy rate should stay 
on hold, given r ising inflation, shrinking bank net interest 
margins (NIMs), and doubts over the effectiveness of modest 
rate cuts when credit demand is weak. 
BoJ to hike policy rate another 25bps to 1.25% by 
December: Japan enters H2  2026 with robust momentum, 
underpinned by a strong external sector. Merchandise exports 
have surged, rising 19.3% y/y in June, led by semiconductor 
manufacturing equipment, integrated circuits and automotive 
shipments to Asia and North America. Manufacturi ng activity 
is expanding, with manufacturing PMI at 54. 7 in Ju ly, a 
seventh straight month of growth, while the services PMI 
printed 51.9, supported by foreign tourism. Corporate capex 
remains resilient, with the Q2 Tankan survey projecting 11.5% 
large-enterprise capex growth for FY26. A tight labour market 
(unemployment rate steady at 2.5%) plus 5.3% total wage  
gains from the 2026 Shunto should lift private consumption.  
With the growth backdrop clearly positive, underlying price 
pressures are building. Although headline consumer inflation 
at 1.7% y/y in June remains below the BoJ's 2.0% target, core-
core inflation of 1. 7% points to persistent services -sector 
pressure. Producer inflation has surged to 7.1% y/y in June, 
its fastest in over three years, driven by energy, chemical and 
imported input costs, amplified by a weak yen. These 
upstream costs are increasingly passing  through to 
consumers. Consumer inflation is likely to rise further once 
government energy subsidies expire. Long -dated inflation 
expectations sit above the BoJ’s target. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.00% in July , but used its 
quarterly outlook to signal a potential rate hike later this year. 
With real rates deeply negative, persistent inflation and  yen 
weakness, the policy tilt remains hawkish, pointing to gradual 
normalisation. The main pushback against rate hikes is 
coming from the government, although yen weakness and a 
government plan to cut a consumption tax on food and 
beverage from 8% to 1% for two years could force a rate hike.

Global Market Outlook 11

Our view

Foundation: We raise fixed income to a Neutral allocation (Core holding) within a balanced portfolio. Within the asset class, 
we maintain an Overweight allocation to EM USD government bonds and an Underweight allocation to DM government 
bonds. We believe peak US inflation is behind us, allowing the Fed to hold rates for the rest of the year. Yields could remain 
elevated, particularly for longer-duration bonds, driven by a high term premium demanded by investors. DM IG and HY are both 
Neutral allocations, as corporate bonds continue to offer carry, though there is limited room for further spread compression. 
Corporate fundamentals remain resilient, with stable leverage and strong interest coverage, supporting our preference for credit 
over rates. In EM, we prefer exposure to USD-denominated government bonds over local-currency (LCY) bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US HY bonds, European bank Additional Tier-1 (AT1) bonds (contingent convertibles 
[CoCos1]; FX-hedged), US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, US utilities sector corporate hybrids and AUD corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We are Underweight DM IG government bonds. In the US, 
the term premium – the compensation demanded by investors 
for holding long -duration bonds  – is expected to remain 
structurally elevated. This is underpinned by a confluence of 
persistent macro headwinds, such as above-target inflation, a 
deteriorating fiscal trajectory, an ongoing uncertainty in the 
Middle East, and more recently , heightened monetary policy 
ambiguity following the Fed’s removal of forward guidance 
and its internal restructuring across five task forces.  
That said, the softer -than-expected June CPI has reinforced 
our conviction that peak US inflation was behind us in Q2, 
providing the Fed ample cover to hold rates through year-end. 
As disinflationary momentum consolidates, we expect the Fed 
to gradually shift its attention towards emerging labour market 
softness – a transition that culminates  in our expectation for 
the Fed to deliver a single 25bps rate cut in H1 2027.  
Against this policy backdrop, we expect the US yield curve to 
transition into a steepening regime (the short-end yield falling 
faster than the long-end yield) over a 12-month horizon, with 
the front-end rallying in anticipation of Fed easing and getting 
increasingly priced , while the long -end anchored under 
structural pressure s. Within this context, we maintain a 
preference for the 3-5-year area of the curve  and retain our 
Underweight to long-duration bonds, where the combination 
of an elevated term premium, fiscal deterioration and subdued 
demand technicals  continues to present an unfavourable 
asymmetry. We set our three- and 12-month forecasts for the 
2-year US government bond yield at 4-4.25% and 3.75 -4%, 
respectively. For the 10-year bond yield, we target 4.50-4.75% 
over three months and 4.25-4.50% over 12 months. 
The Euro area continues to face a more challenging inflation 
backdrop, driven by elevated energy prices, given its status 
as a large commodity importer. Economic data has been 
weakening, and we expect the ECB to deliver another 
‘insurance hike’ of 25bps before year-end, following the 25bp 
hike in June. Should economic conditions deteriorate , the 
central bank could react promptly by easing monetary policy.

We maintain a  Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM 
HY corporate bonds. While we acknowledge there is limited 
room for corporate spreads to compress materially from 
current levels – especially with US IG index spreads around 
80bps – we expect dispersion to increase from here.

Global Market Outlook 13 
Our Overweight EM USD government bond stance is 
supported by a 2% -plus yield pick -up vs. DM bonds, 
resilient external fundamentals and favourable 
technicals. EM USD sovereigns also benefit from a more oil-
resilient index composition, with non -oil importers 
representing 67% of exposure vs . 46% for EM LCY debt, 
providing a relative buffer against oil-price shocks. Concerns 
that heavy US tech -sector issuance could crowd out EM 
demand appear overdone, with history suggesting EM IG 
sovereigns have often prove n resilient and could even 
outperform if pressure on US IG spreads persists. 
On LCY, EM FX has recoupled with the USD , and volatility 
remains above its 10-year average. As a result, alpha is likely 
to come from country selection rather than broad beta 
exposure. The main risks to our view are a prolonged Middle 
East conflict escalation driving oil sharply higher, a hawkish 
Fed surprise and a stronger El Niño impulse into H2 2026. 
Asia USD bonds – Core holding 
We retain a Neutral allocation to Asia USD bonds, an asset 
class that continues to reward investors with attractive 
nominal yields, favourable supply -demand technicals and 
robust credit fundamentals, underpinned by healthy cash  
flows, low leverage and a high share of sovereign -linked 
issuers that lend the segment a defensive quality.  
This resilience has been evident YTD, with Asia USD  bonds 
delivering positive total returns, achieving modest spread 
tightening relative to US government bonds, and 
outperforming US IG bonds. We believe this relative strength 
can persist, as Asia USD bonds remain structurally insulated 
from the currency headwinds currently weighing on currency 
markets across the region, which anchors our conviction in the 
asset class even as we maintain a Neutral stance. 
 Bond opportunistic views 
Bullish US HY bonds 
We expect HY corporate earnings and cash flows to remain 
solid and leverage to remain stable in a soft -landing 
environment. The overall default rate has been below trend 
and remains supportive of stable carry. Active refinancing has 
pushed the maturity wall into 2028-29. 
Bullish US inflation-protected bonds (TIPS) 
Real yields have surged to multi -decade highs , driven by 
elevated rate hike expectations  and lower inflation 
expectations post the June US-Iran ceasefire. We believe 
valuations appear more attractive compared to the historical 
average and view TIPS as an inflation hedge against energy 
prices and fiscal concerns. They should benefit from lower 
yields if the Fed resumes rate cuts in H2 2026.   
Bullish utilities corporate hybrids 
AI capex and related energy demand are poised to continue 
in the US, benefiting US utilities. While utilities will also need 
to expand capex, we believe credit fundamentals will remain 
stable in 2026 as revenues grow. We prefer hybrids over  
senior bonds for yield enhancement. Hybrids’ non -call risk 
should remain low, supported by organic cash  flows and 
diversified financing channels.

Bullish Australian dollar (AUD) corporate 
bonds 
Following three consecutive rate hikes, we believe the 
threshold for further RBA hikes has risen , largely due to the 
drag on  growth from higher energy prices . Recent data 
reaffirms our view that inflation has likely peaked. While 
housing remains a key contributor to inflation, we expect its 
impact to wane as tighter financial condition s take hold. We 
view AUD yields as attractive, particularly relative to major 
developed markets.  
Bullish EU bank CoCos1 (FX-hedged) 
European bank sector fundamentals remain solid, as denoted 
by ample liquidity coverage, strong capital buffers and still -
supportive asset quality. We believe CoCos will benefit from 
the current late-cycle environment. 
Bullish AAA-rated CLOs 
Private credit spillover concerns are mounting. However, we 
believe high-quality CLOs backed by solid asset portfolios and 
gaining exposure via rigorous asset manager screening 
should help navigate the volatility.
